### Page 1

NYPCB

Alarm cleared with new drivers into 2027; 
re-it. Buy with PO raise to NT$2,000

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 2,000 TWD | Price: 1,340 TWD

BT substrate price hike resumed from 4Q26 
Following (see report) the worries of moderating BT substrate price hike in early 
September, we now expect NYPCB to still raise relevant ASP by single-digit % in 4Q26, 
and the hike is purely driven by favorable S/D which will fully translate into the accretion 
in bottom-line. Following this, BT substrate price will likely be raised by another 20+% in 
1Q27, due partly to the cost pass-through. We currently bake in ~10% revenue QoQ with 
32-33% GM for 4Q26, but we do not rule out the possibility for its revenue to surprise 
on the upside, should there be a faster ramp of its ABF substrate capacity in Kunshan. 
New driver for 2027: server CPU & mix optimization  
Looking into 2027 we expect the benefits of demand overflow on (see report) server 
CPU side to materialize, and we flag a chance for the firm to record 20+% revenue from 
the emerging business with AMD server CPU, where NYPCB is working on the 
qualification in its Shulin site currently and should capture most of the spillover demand 
if the progress goes smooth. Meanwhile, the firm will likely allocate more capacity from 
other sites (e.g. Kunshan) to fulfill the demand from Broadcom, which also helps enhance 
the mix. Currently we model ~50% GM for its ABF substrate business in 2027, but we 
see chance for further upward revision, driven by more favorable S/D dynamics and mix.  
Raise estimates/PO; prime beneficiary of price hike 
NYPCB’s share price (up ~50%) lags its major peers Unimicron/Kinsus (up ~70%) since 
late-July, but in our view it is still the best proxy to position for price hike in the 
substrate market. Judging from (see report) Kinsus’ August prints last week, we believe 
NYPCB remains on track to meet or beat the market’s expectations on N-T margins, and 
we reiterate Buy on the stock. Along with 13%/18% EPS raise in 2027/28E thanks to the 
more benign pricing/margin outlook driven by further mix improvement and S/D, we 
raise PO to NT$2,000 (from NT$1,700) same based on 35x 2H27-1H28E P/E.

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
Founded in 1997, NYPCB was listed on the Taiwan Stock 
Exchange in 2006. The firm is a 67%-owned subsidiary of 
Nan Ya Plastics Corporation (1303 TT). NYPCB's main 
products are HDI, PCB and IC substrates (including ABF and 
BT substrates), widely used in telecommunication, 
consumers, PC, and Auto applications. The clients include 
AMD, Broadcom, Samsung, Apple, Continental, etc. The 
main competitors include Unimicron, Kinsus, Ibiden, 
Shinko, and SEMCO, etc.

Investment Rationale 
We have a Buy rating on NYPCB, in view of its continuous 
improvement of utilization rate and gross margin across 
ABF and BT substrate business into 2027, thanks to the 
robust demand growth from fabless customers and 
demand overflow in AI ASICs.

### Page 5

Price objective basis & risk 
NYPCB (NANYF) 
Our PO of NT$2,000 is based on 35x 2H27-1H28E P/E. We view this multiple as fair 
given ROE and operating margin expansion to 39% and 41% in 2027-28E (vs 13% and 
14% in 2025-26E).

Upside risks to our PO are (1) tighter ABF supply, (2) higher SiP/AiP adoption, and (3) 
less intensified competition pressure in PCB business.

Downside risks are (1) greater ABF expansion from peers, (2) muted SiP/AiP adoption, 
and (3) more severe competition in PCB business.

iQmethod SMis the set of BofA Global Research standard measures that serve to maintain global consistency under three broad headings: Business Performance, Quality of Earnings, and validations. The key features of 
iQmethod are: A consistently structured, detailed, and transparent methodology. Guidelines to maximize the effectiveness of the comparative valuation process, and to identify some common pitfalls. 
iQdatabase ®  is our real-time global research database that is sourced directly from our equity analysts’ earnings models and includes forecasted as well as historical data for income statements, balance sheets, and cash 
flow statements for companies covered by BofA Global Research. 
iQprofile SM, iQmethod SM  are service marks of Bank of America Corporation. iQdatabase ® is a registered service mark of Bank of America Corporation.

R1 Issuers that were investment banking clients of BofA Securities or one of its affiliates within the past 12 months. For purposes of this Investment Rating Distribution, the coverage universe includes only stocks. A stock

FUNDAMENTAL EQUITY OPINION KEY: Opinions include a Volatility Risk Rating, an Investment Rating and an Income Rating. VOLATILITY RISK RATINGS, indicators 
of potential price fluctuation, are: A - Low, B - Medium and C - High. INVESTMENT RATINGS reflect the analyst’s assessment of both a stock’s absolute total return 
potential as well as its attractiveness for investment relative to other stocks within its Coverage Cluster (defined below). Our investment rating s are: 1 - Buy stocks 
are expected to have a total return of at least 10% and are the most attractive stocks in the coverage cluster; 2 - Neutral stocks are expected to remain flat or 
increase in value and are less attractive than Buy rated stocks and 3 - Underperform stocks are the least attractive stocks in a coverage cluster. An investment 
rating of 6 (No Rating) indicates that a stock is no longer trading on the basis of fundamentals. Analysts assign investment ratings considering, among other things, 
the 0-12 month total return expectation for a stock and the firm’s guidelines for ratings dispersions (shown in the table below). The current price objective for a 
stock should be referenced to better understand the total return expectation at any given time. The price objective reflects the analyst’s view of the potential price 
appreciation (depreciation). 
Investment rating Total return expectation (within 12-month period of date of initial 
rating) 
Ratings dispersion guidelines for coverage clusterR2 
Buy ≥ 10% ≤ 70% 
Neutral ≥ 0% ≤ 30% 
Underperform N/A ≥ 20% 
R2Ratings dispersions may vary from time to time where BofA Global Research believes it better reflects the investment prospects of stocks in a Coverage Cluster. 
INCOME RATINGS, indicators of potential cash dividends, are: 7 - same/higher (dividend considered to be secure), 8 - same/lower (dividend not considered to be 
secure) and 9 - pays no cash dividend.  Coverage Cluster is comprised of stocks covered by a single analyst or two  or more analysts sharing a common industry,

Hon Hai (2317.TW): Sep beat: revenues +38% YoY; New AI servers, CPO 
switch and Foldable phones to drive 4Q26; Buy (on CL)
5 October 2026 | 11:23PM HKT
Hon Hai’s Sep revenues were +38% YoY/ 26% MoM to NT$1.2tn, or 15% / 21% 
higher than our / Bloomberg consensus estimates, reflecting strong AI servers ramp 
up and new smartphone models in contribution. From a MoM perspective, 
Consumer electronics is the strongest, followed by Components, Cloud and 
Networking and Computing, and all segments are positive MoM. The strong MoM 
reaffirms our positive view on the company’s leading market position across: (1) 
Smartphone: serving global leading brands, which suffer less under high memory 
costs, with new model launches and form factor changes (report link), driving the 
shipment and ASP increase, (2) AI servers: serving global leading CSP customers, 
and early in the delivery of the latest AI server racks, driving the shipment and ASP 
increase. The company is also the major supplier of CPO switch, supporting Sep and 
4Q26 growth. From a YoY perspective, Cloud and Networking is the strongest, 
followed by Components, Computing, and Consumer electronics, and all segments 
are positive YoY. We attribute the slower YoY in consumer electronics to its major 
brand customers’ fewer model launches this year, along with the new form factor 
(foldable phones) being not available in Sep, and a modest smartphone market 
under high memory costs.

3-month revenues preview: We expect company’s 4Q26E revenues to be +37% 
YoY/ +18% QoQ to NT$3.6 trillion. 3Q26 delivered strong revenues YoY growth of 
47%, or 5% / 7% ahead of our / Bloomberg consensus, and we expect to see both 
YoY and MoM growth throughout Oct-Dec, driven by new AI server racks, new 
smartphone models, and CPO switches. Management notes that AI-related products

EV business in Sep 2026: Hon Hai’s EV subsidiary Foxtron (2258.TW, not covered) 
reported Sep revenues of NT$1.1bn (vs. Aug-26 / Sep-25 revenues at NT$914mn / 
NT$242mn). In Sep 2026, Taiwan’s new vehicle registrations rebounded to 37k units, 
while EV registrations delivered robust sequential momentum, more than doubling MoM 
to 7,026 units (vs. 3,259 units in August). Notably, Foxtron ranked No.2 with cumulative 
YTD sales volume reaching 3,138 units (news).

Earnings revision: We factor in Hon Hai Sep revenues and revise up 2026E net income 
by 2% to reflect the strong beat, mainly on higher revenues. We remain positive on AI 
server racks ramp up, Hon Hai’s leading market position in terms of scale, technology, 
and execution, and rising foldable phones to drive growth ahead. We keep 2027E/ 
2028E estimates largely unchanged.

Valuation: Our 12m TP for Hon Hai is unchanged at NT$400 and remains based on a 
P/E. Our target 2027E P/E multiple of 17.9x (unchanged) is derived from a peers’ 
average ratio of trading P/E to forward year NI YoY growth and OPM (P/E divided by the 
sum of NI YoY and OPM). Our target P/E of 17.9x is at the high-end of the company’s 
historical forward P/E, reflecting our positive view on Hon Hai’s business expansion from 
the competitive consumer electronics market to AI servers and EVs outsourcing, which 
should drive profit expansion with diversifying business opportunities for Hon Hai. 
Maintain Buy (on CL).

Price Target Risks and Methodology – Hon Hai 
Valuation methodology: We are Buy rated on Hon Hai. Our 12-month target price of 
NT$400 is based on an 17.9x 2027E P/E multiple, which is set in line with peers’ PEG&M 
ratio (i.e., P/E vs. forward-year earnings growth and OPM). 
Key downside risks: (1) slower-than-expected ramp-up of the AI server business; (2) 
weaker-than-expected EV total solution performance across EV assembly, design,

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

休閒車新訂單與電子新產品挹注成長 
 2H26 持續受惠美國 Honda 新訂單放量出貨 ， 以及越南電動機車客戶
產能供不應求的外包訂單需求 ， 營收可望逐季墊高 。 推估下半年平均
每月營收 8~8.5 億元。 
 休閒車產品 2026 年在客戶 Polaris 調整告一段落以及北美 Honda 與
哈雷新訂單開始挹注下，預期營收貢獻將成長 50%以上，2027 年北
美 Honda 還有其他車型將量產，推估可再成長 20-30%。 
 相關機櫃與散熱產品將於 2Q26 陸續少量出貨 ，2027 年將放量出貨 。

線需求越南百善二廠二期擴建，預計 3Q27 末完工，推估 2027 年營
收比重可達近雙位數。

Honda 與哈雷新訂單挹注。預期 2026 年 EPS 17.25 元。2027 年休
閒車新訂單挹注以及電子產品隨著產能開出放量 ， 預期EPS 21.3 元。 
 維持買進 ：公司受惠北美 HONDA 新訂單與電動機車客戶轉單挹
注，今明兩年營運轉好。此外，受惠供應鏈由中國轉移至越南，公司
越南廠具在地優勢取得電子新訂單 ， 隨著越南百善二廠廠能開出 ， 將
挹注未來成長動能。維持買進建議，目標價 234 元。

個股報告

北美新訂單挹注，營運逐季墊高

越南電動二輪政策需求， 彌補燃
油機車缺口

公司 2Q26 營收 24.1 億元 ，+16.9%QoQ，+53.1%YoY， 主要為休閒車輛訂單回溫 ，
加上越南機車業務拉貨回升。在稼動率提升與產品結構轉好下，毛利率23.8%較上
季好轉，單季 EPS 4.42 元。

2H26 持續受惠美國 Honda 新訂單放量出貨 ， 以及越南電動機車客戶產能供不應求
的外包訂單需求 ， 營收可望逐季墊高 。 推估下半年平均每月營收8~8.5 億元 ，3Q26
營收 24.34 億元，+1%QoQ，+58.8%YoY，EPS 4.72 元。4Q26 為越南機車產業
旺季，預計營收 25.07 億元，+3%QoQ，+38.9%YoY，EPS 4.53 元。

1H26 主要產品營收佔比依序為機車 44.8%、 休閒車43.9%、 健康8.6%、 汽車2.6%。

機車產品方面，越南傳統機車市場為主，主要客戶為 YAMAHA 與 Honda，2025
年受到 HONDA 下修生產計劃影響，營收貢獻較前一年下滑 14%。2026 年越南河
內與胡志明市將禁止傳統燃油二輪，轉為發展電動二輪市場，雖影響傳統燃油機車
市場，但受惠政策，新的電動二輪換車需求彌補了油車下滑的缺口。  
目前客戶 Vinfast 產能吃緊釋出訂單 ，1H26 機車產品營收貢獻成長 22%， 預期2026
年機車營收可成長 16-17%。2027 年越南 Honda 將有電動二輪產品推出，有利維
持機車產品成長。

北美 Honda 與哈雷為休閒車產品
主要成長動能

汽車與健康事業轉佳

休閒車：主要客戶為 Polaris、Harley 與北美 Honda，受惠客戶為降低成本與避開
當地關稅的策略，將產線移至越南，以及美國當地非紅供應鏈政策，相關供應鏈由
中國轉向越南。而 2025 年在川普總統推動美國製造下，公司調整產線並於美國南
卡羅來納州設廠因應。2025 年受到通膨影響北美休閒車市場需求趨緩，主要客戶
Polaris 調整庫存，目前庫存水位已將至低位，自 1Q26 客戶陸續補庫存。

新訂單方面，北美 Honda ATV 零件，目前接單規劃至 2028 年都有新專案出貨。
由於 Honda ATV 在北美規模與 Polaris 相當，為未來成長的主要客戶與動能，新
訂單於 2Q26 量產，預估營收貢獻將從 2025 年的 4.9 億元大幅增至 2026 年的 
12~13 億元。此外，公司哈雷訂單於 1Q26 開始出貨，推估 2026 年可貢獻 9-10
億元，目前仍有新項目在開發，將挹注未來營運動能。

休閒車產品 2026 年在客戶 Polaris 調整告一段落以及北美 Honda 與哈雷新訂單開
始挹注下，預期營收貢獻將成長 50%以上。2027 年除了北美 Honda SXS 與 ATV
訂單外，還有其他車型將量產，加上哈雷，與歐洲、日本的新訂單，推估休閒車營
收貢獻可再成長 20-30%。

汽車相關產品取得 Toyota、Honda 與 Vinfast 電動車訂單，加上其他車廠未來也
規劃將組裝線設於越南， 公司看好未來越南與東協車用市場成長， 透過與相關供應
鏈的台商合資取得供應商資格，未來將可從業外認列轉投資收益。 2025 年受到越
南需求不如預期影響 ， 營收貢獻僅成長6%，2026 年越南車用市場回穩 ， 加上Vinfast

需求增加，營收貢獻成長 45%。

健康事業目前包含醫療、健身器材、E-bike 等產品，先前健身器材新產品雖取得客
戶認證，但出貨時間遞延，2025 年營收貢獻下滑 17-18%，目前開始小量出貨，
在基期較低下，預期 2026 年營收貢獻成長 25%。

公司於越南設立子公司，將所有電子相關客戶集中生產。產品為伺服器機櫃、散熱
產品與伺服器內部零部件，相關機櫃與散熱產品將於 2Q26 陸續少量出貨，2027
年將放量出貨。預估電子產品營收佔比年底可增至 3-5%。因應電子與大型自動化
產線需求越南百善二廠二期擴建，預計 3Q27 末完工，推估 2027 年營收比重可達
近雙位數。

結 構 的 實 質 計 算 上 ， 仲 裁 庭 所 認 定 的 3 大 賠 償 項 目 原 本 已 具 備 明 確 數 字 ，

億元。

商，雙方具備共同商業利益， 不排除 最終走向和解或重新協議供貨條件的

前 8 月 累 計 營 收 已 達 新 台 幣 171 億元 ， 一 次 性 認 列 約 36 億元 ( 或者更高 ) 損
失 衝擊 ， 不至 於 構 成 資 產 負 債 表 等 級 的 營 運 生 存 危 機 。 
最 關 鍵 的 成 長 動 能 來 自美 國 市 場，包 含 真 性 紅 血 球 增 多 症 PV 放 量 ， 以 及新

3 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】 
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy)  5%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等 
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整 
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】 
  投 資 評 等 係 「 首 次 」 給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時 ， 其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較3 個
月目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為 四 個等級，定義如
上 。 爾 後 的 投 資 評 等係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」及「 區 間 操 作 」均有 upside 目 標 價 。差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。 
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」 或「區間操作」 之投資評
等，就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基
本 面 及 股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。 
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ： 
1.  達 目 標 價 。 
2.  雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ，
研 判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。 
3.  推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 ， 亦 將 出 具 降 評 報 告 。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」。
我們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來
預測之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投
資 目 的 或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的 ，
既非對投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提
供詢價服務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值
及收益，可能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投
資 來 得 少 的 情 形 。

### Page 1

宏观经济 | 证券研究报告 — 总量点评 2026 年 10 月 8 日

《海外宏观研究专题之一》20261007

中银国际证券股份有限公司

9 月 PMI 数据点评

建筑业景气度回升至扩张区间   
9 月制造业生产指数走高， 需求整体仍位于扩张区间。9 月建筑业景气度年内
首次回升至扩张区间。 
◼ 制造业生产指数走高。9 月制造业 PMI 指数为 50.1%， 环比 （8 月， 下同）
上升 0.3 个百分点，制造业景气度回升至扩张区间。 
制造业需求整体仍位于扩张区间。着眼重要细分项， 9 月新订单指数
50.5%， 环比下滑0.1 个百分点；9 月新出口订单指数环比回落 0.1 个百分
点，当月值为 50.0%；9 月产成品库存指数 47.6%，环比下滑 0.8 个百分
点； 制造业需求整体仍位于扩张区间。值得一提的是，9 月非制造业中建
筑业 PMI 指数为 50.3%，年内首次回升至扩张区间，固定资产投资发力
或是 9 月国内需求的重要支撑。 
◼ 9 月非制造业 PMI 指数回升至扩张区间。9 月非制造业 PMI（商务活动）
指数为 50.2%，环比上月走高 1.2 个百分点。从需求端看，9 月新订单指
数为 46.5%， 环比走高2.4 百分点。 外需方面， 新出口订单指数为48.5%，
环比上月走高 1.5 个百分点。9 月非制造业从业人员指数为 45.9%，环比
上月走高 0.5 个百分点， 但仍处于较深的收缩区间。供应商配送时间指数
51.3%，环比回升 0.1 个百分点。 
◼ 9 月建筑业景气度年内首次回升至扩张区间。 建筑业PMI 指数 50.3%，环
比回升 3.4 个百分点，建筑业景气度年内首次回升至扩张区间。9 月新订
单指数 45.7%，环比走高 3.3 个百分点。从业人员指数 44.3%，环比改善
1.3 个百分点。此外，业务活动预期指数 55.4%，环比改善 3.6 个百分点，
9 月建筑业多个分项指标表现改善， 新订单、 从业人员指数及业务活动预
期均有回升，或能在一定程度上反映出 9 月固定资产投资进度加快。 
◼ 服务业景气度回升至扩张区间。9 月服务业 PMI 指数 50.2%，环比回升
0.9 个百分点，服务业景气度回升至扩张区间。新订单指数 46.7%，环比
回升 2.2 个百分点；销售价格指数环比回升 1.4 个百分点，至 50.6%；从
业人员指数环比回升 0.4 个百分点，为 46.2%。 
◼ 风险提示：海外大宗商品价格震荡；地缘关系的不确定性；政策效果不
及预期。

### Page 2

2026 年 10 月 8 日 9 月 PMI 数据点评 2

9 月制造业生产指数走高，需求整体仍位于扩张区间 
制造业生产指数走高。9 月制造业 PMI 指数为 50.1%，环比（8 月，下同）上升 0.3 个百分点，制造
业景气度回升至扩张区间。 
分产业看，9 月高技术制造业 PMI 为 52.5%，环比下滑 0.4 个百分点，但仍高于制造业总体水平，
且维持在较高景气度区间，仍是当前制造业景气度回升的重要驱动；原材料行业 PMI 为 48.0%，仍
处于收缩区间。值得一提的是，9 月消费品行业 PMI 为 50.7%，环比上升 1.7 个百分点， 行业景气度
回升至扩张区间，或受到中秋、国庆假期因素带动；此外，9 月装备制造业 PMI 为 51.0%，较 8 月
小幅下滑 0.4 个百分点，但仍然维持在扩张区间；我们认为，9 月高技术制造业、装备制造业及消费
品行业均受假期因素干扰，四季度扩内需政策发力或仍对相关产业景气度有所带动。 
制造业需求整体仍位于扩张区间 。着眼重要细分项，9 月新订单指数 50.5%，环比下滑 0.1 个百分点；
9 月新出口订单指数环比回落 0.1 个百分点，当月值为 50.0%；9 月产成品库存指数 47.6%，环比下
滑 0.8 个百分点；制造业需求整体仍位于扩张区间。值得一提的是，9 月非制造业中建筑业 PMI 指
数为 50.3%，年内首次回升至扩张区间，固定资产投资发力或是 9 月国内需求的重要支撑。 
9 月生产指数 51.7%，环比回升 1.3 个百分点；原材料库存指数 48.2%，环比回升 0.1 个百分点；从
业人员指数 48.4 %，环比下滑 0.3 个百分点； 供货商配送时间指数50.1%，与 8 月持平。 企业预期方
面，制造业生产经营活动预期指数 53.8%，持平于 8 月，制造业经营预期维持在较高景气度区间。
从生产相关的细分指标看，月内在手订单和采购指数环比分别变动-0.7 和+0.5 个百分点。 
9 月生产指数环比走高，制造业采购备货意愿较强，且加速消化在手订单，或在一定程度上受到假
期因素影响，中秋、国庆假期时间较长，企业赶工意愿或较强。

图表 1. 制造业 PMI 与历史同期相比（%）

资料来源：wind，中银证券

价格指数方面，9 月主要原材料购进价格指数 60.8%,环比明显走高 4.2 百分点， 在较高景气区间继续
增长；制造业出厂价格指数也在扩张区间继续走高，环比增长 3.6 个百分点，当月值为 54.0%。 
综合来看，首先，9 月主要原材料购进价格指数涨幅仍然大于出厂价格指数，上游成本端推动或仍
是月内价格指数回升的重要因素。其次，需求回升对 9 月出厂价格指数的表现或仍存在支撑；9 月
新订单指数环比微幅回落，但仍维持在扩张区间，且在一定程度上受到外需拖累；同期产成品库存
指数下滑 0.8 个百分点，需求韧性带动产成品库存消化，制造业“被动去库存”现象仍存，下游或
仍具备一定的议价能力。

重点行业盈利能力方面，9 月专用设备制造业短期、长期盈利能力均较领先，或得益于 9 月建筑业
景气度回升；同期汽车制造业短期、长期盈利能力也在各行业中较为领先，汽车产业链盈利能力延
续改善；此外，计算机通信电子设备及仪器仪表制造业 短、长期盈利能力在各行业中均处于中等偏
上位置。长期盈利方面，有色金属冶炼及压延加工业盈利领先于其他行业。

大型制造企业支撑继续凸显。9 月大型企业 PMI 为 50.6%，持平于 8 月，仍处于扩张区间；中、小
型制造企业 PMI 分别为 49.7%和 48.9%，环比分别回升 0.3 和 1.0 个百分点，中小企业景气度均有改
善，但仍处于收缩区间。

9 月非制造业 PMI 指数回升至扩张区间 
9 月非制造业 PMI 指数回升至扩张区间。9 月非制造业 PMI（商务活动）指数为 50.2%，环比上月走
高 1.2 个百分点。从需求端看，9 月新订单指数为 46.5%，环比走高 2.4 百分点。外需方面，新出口
订单指数为 48.5%，环比上月走高 1.5 个百分点。9 月非制造业从业人员指数为 45.9%，环比上月走
高 0.5 个百分点，但仍处于较深的收缩区间。供应商配送时间指数 51.3%，环比回升 0.1 个百分点。

9 月建筑业景气度年内首次回升至扩张区间。建筑业 PMI 指数 50.3%，环比回升 3.4 个百分点，建
筑业景气度年内首次回升至扩张区间。9 月新订单指数 45.7%，环比走高 3.3 个百分点。从业人员指
数 44.3%，环比改善 1.3 个百分点。此外，业务活动预期指数 55.4%，环比改善 3.6 个百分点，9 月
建筑业多个分项指标表现改善，新订单、从业人员指数及业务活动预期均有回升，或能在一定程度
上反映出 9 月固定资产投资进度加快。 
服务业景气度回升至扩张区间。9 月服务业 PMI 指数 50.2%，环比回升 0.9 个百分点， 服务业景气度
回升至扩张区间。 新订单指数46.7%，环比回升 2.2 个百分点； 销售价格指数环比回升 1.4 个百分点，
至 50.6%；从业人员指数环比回升 0.4 个百分点，为 46.2%。

评级体系说明 
以报告发布日后公司股价/行业指数涨跌幅相对同期相关市场指数的涨跌幅的表现为基准：  
公司投资评级： 
买    入：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 20%以上； 
增    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 10%-20%； 
中    性：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数变动幅度在-10%-10%之间； 
减    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数跌幅在 10%以上； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

行业投资评级： 
强于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现强于基准指数； 
中    性：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现基本与基准指数持平； 
弱于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现弱于基准指数； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

沪深市场基准指数为沪深 300 指数；新三板市场基准指数为三板成指或三板做市指数；香港市场基准指数为恒生指数
或恒生中国企业指数；美股市场基准指数为纳斯达克综合指数或标普 500 指数。

## Document 113: 9月美国非农数据点评：加息或暂缓而非结束

Source PDF: all report pdf/9月美国非农数据点评：加息或暂缓而非结束.pdf
Report source: Unknown

Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

此报告最后部分的分析师披露、商业关系披露和免责声明为报告的一部分，必须阅读。
下载本公司之研究报告，可从彭博搜寻NH BCM  或 登录研究部网站https://research.bocomgroup.com 
交银国际研究 
宏观策略

2026年10月6日
全球宏观 
加息或暂缓而非结束——9月美国非农数据点评
9月美国新增非农数据偏弱，前个两月的数据也下修。美国9月新增非农
2.9万，市场预期增加9万。之前数据修正也以下修为主，8月数据第一次
修正下修2.9万人到增加13.3万人，7月数据第二次修正下修至减少1万
人，但相对于初值上修1.3万人。
季调可能也有干扰，9月非季调的新增非农仍略强于去年。非季调的非农
9月增加了33.3万人，去年9月增加30.5万人。经季调后新增非农的2.9
万人则低于去年同期修正后的7.6万人。
9月美国失业率小幅上升，但劳动参与率回升。9月美国失业率从4.1%小
幅上升到4.2%。对比去年9月的4.4%。从周期角度看，目前失业率水平仍
处于较低水平，远未达到任何历史衰退时期的水平。同时失业率的上升部
分来自于分母变化，劳动参与率由61.6%进一步上升至61.8%，本月失业
率上升并非主要由裁员推动，而是更多人口重新进入劳动力市场。
9月美国工资压力环比略有缓解，平均时薪同比增速仍下降。9月美国私
营非农企业全体雇员平均时薪环比上涨0.1%，同比涨幅为3.0%，低于8月
同比的3.1%。平均每周工时维持在34.4小时。
最新高频数据显示美国失业水平仍处于相对低位。截至9月26日当周，季
调后美国初次申领失业金人数为19.7万人，略低于上一周，近4周平均
20.0万人，仍处于相对低位。与此同时，最新公布的8月JOLTS数据显示，
招聘率略有回升，但仍距离过热水平较远，辞职率则变化不大，维持低
位。
我们预期加息周期并未结束，但节奏或有调整，美联储可能10月暂缓加
息。数据公布前，利率期货市场显示10月加息概率不到3成，12月加息
是基准情形；数据公布后，10月加息概率进一步降至仅约2成。尽管9月
美国非农数据偏弱，但是尚不足以令美联储就此结束加息周期，我们认
为，美联储大概率继续等待更多数据，尤其是通胀数据公布后再做决策。
我们维持此前预计美联储年内可能再加息一次的观点，加息时点倾向于
12月。
美债收益率先降后升，美股三大股指上行。10月2日，美国三大股指均上
涨，其中纳斯达克领涨1.19%，标普上涨0.73%，道琼斯指数上涨0.49%。
分行业看，标普500行业指数中仅医疗保健下跌，其他行业指数均上涨，
其中非必需消费、信息技术和材料板块涨幅靠前。美国国债收益率盘中一
度下行，但收盘普遍略微上行，我们认为偏弱的就业数据降低了近期加息
预期，但尚未显著改变市场对中期通胀和利率维持高位的预期。10年期
国债收益率全日上升4个基点，1年期收益率利率上升2个基点，3个月国
债收益率上升2个基点。美元指数当日跌约0.17%。

评级定义
分析员个股评级定义 : 分析员行业评级定义：
买入：预期个股未来12个月的总回报高于相关行业。
中性：预期个股未来12个月的总回报与相关行业一致。
沽出：预期个股未来12个月的总回报低于相关行业
无评级：对于个股未来12个月的总回报与相关行业的比较，
分析员并无确信观点。
领先：分析员预期所覆盖行业未来12个月的表现相对于大盘
标杆指数具吸引力。
同步：分析员预期所覆盖行业未来12个月的表现与大盘标杆
指数一致。
落后：分析员预期所覆盖行业未来12个月的表现相对于大盘
标杆指数不具吸引力。
香港市场的标杆指数为恒生综合指数，A股市场的标杆指数

## Document 144: AI驅動高速互聯，矽光子落地，CPO續章 202609

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI驅動高速互聯，矽光子落地，CPO續章 202609.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 32
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

AI
驅動
高速互
聯，矽光子落地，
CPO
續章
福邦投顧 研究部
2026/09

### Page 2

2【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
結論
⚫ AI算力需求推升高速光互聯市場。美國四大CSP持續擴大AI資料中心建置，為帶動高速光
模組需求成長的主要動能。現階段高速光模組出貨仍以800G為主，1.6T預計自2027年逐步
放量，也帶動矽光(SiPh)模組滲透率提高。
⚫ 矽光模組非新興產品，外型仍沿用既有可插拔光模組規格，但相較傳統光模組，SiPh具備
元件高度整合及晶圓級量產優勢，隨高速傳輸所需而獲廣泛採用。然而，雷射晶片受限於
上游InP基板缺貨而供應吃緊。為確保貨源，部分業者已透過長約或預付款方式鎖定供應，
使掌握貨源者在市場上更具競爭優勢。
⚫ CPO現階段仍面臨光耦合、測試與量產等製程挑戰；對CSP而言，CPO改變其既有可插拔光
模組的維修與更換模式，加上NPO、XPO等替代架構持續發展，使CPO雖已進入量產階段
，短期滲透率仍偏低。展望未來，傳輸頻寬持續提升，為降低傳輸損耗與功率，光引擎將
逐步靠近交換器ASIC或加速卡，CPO仍具導入需求。此外，NVIDIA持續擴大NVLink

Fusion生態系，擴大CPO潛在應用市場，惟規模部署時程仍待帶動頻寬需求的新應用興起。
⚫ 相關個股：聯亞(3081)、IET-KY(4971)、寰宇-KY(4991)、聯鈞(3450)、訊芯-KY(6451)、華
星光(4979)、光聖(6442)、波若威(3163)、上詮(3363)、大立光(3008)

### Page 4

AI算力需求推升高速光互聯市場

### Page 5

5【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
AI DC建置，光通訊需求持續成長
• 生成式AI浪潮帶動美國CSP進入DC建置競賽，2023年仍為投資初期，2024年CapEx明顯加速
，預期2026年美國四大CSP—Amazon、Google、微軟、Meta ，CapEx合計增至7,500億美元，
帶動高速光互聯(networking)需求。
• Amazon更指出，預期AWS算力供不應求情況將延續至2027年，且2028年需求能見度已高。
• 2022年底ChatGPT推出帶動生成式AI浪潮，惟當時COVID-19疫情剛結束，Amazon和Meta因
疫情期間大幅擴張基礎建設而下調2023年CapEx，使四大CSP整體CapEx較2022年略低。
資料來源：福邦投顧彙整

### Page 6

6【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
預估2031年光模組市場營收將達1,120億美元
資料來源：Yole, 福邦投顧彙整
• 市調機構預估，至2031年光模組市場營收將達1,120億美元，CAGR成長30%。AI DC為營收
貢獻主力， 佔比達9成。
• 2025年合計資料中心與電信商的光模組營收為230億美元，當時兩者營收分別為180億與50億
美元，差異有限；2031年資料中心的光模組營收達1,010億，電信商僅增為110億美元。
圖2、2025與2031年光模組市場規模比較

### Page 7

7【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
光互連應用由機櫃延伸至跨資料中心
資料來源：Ciena, 福邦投顧彙整
圖3、資料中心Scale Up、Scale Out與Scale Across架構
• 目前光通訊產品出貨集中於Scale Up與Scale Out應用；隨資料中心建置規模擴大及耗電量增加
，受限於電力與土地供給，資料中心朝分散式建置發展，並透過光纖串聯多座資料中心以進行
共同運算，帶動Scale Across需求。
• Scale Up為機櫃內元件與設備互連；Scale Out為資料中心內不同機櫃間互連；Scale Across為
資料中心互連。

### Page 8

8【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
依傳輸距離採用不同光互聯技術
資料來源：Ciena, 福邦投顧彙整
• Scale Up傳輸距離較短，以成本較低的主動銅纜(ACC)為主；隨傳輸速率增加，銅線難以維
持訊號品質，便會採用光纖兩端配置光收發模組的主動光纜(AOC)。
• Scale Out主流採用IMDD可插拔(pluggable)光模組。
• NPO和CPO為應用於Scale Out和Scale up的新技術，藉縮短光引擎與交換器ASIC距離以降低
高速傳輸的電訊號損耗。NPO的光引擎鄰近交換器ASIC擺放；CPO的光引擎與交換器ASIC
共同封裝。
• Scale Across傳輸距離約20公里以上，需採用相干光(coherent)技術來維持訊號品質，供應商
相對有限，以歐美系業者為主。
圖4、三類架構的光互聯技術需求
Scale UpScale OutScale Across
ACC及AOCIMDD可插拔光模組
不可插拔相干光模組
可插拔相干光模組
NPO及CPO

矽光子技術與供應鏈發展

### Page 10

10【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
AI資料中心的光互聯台廠供應鏈
• AI DC 高速互連升級，帶動 AOC/ACC 與800G/1.6T可插拔光模組需求成長，並推升矽光
(SiPh)模組滲透率。台廠AOC供應商包括佳必琪與源傑。
• 台廠受惠範圍由光模組元件與封裝，進一步延伸至CW Laser、光學次模組封裝及 PIC 代工
• 高密度光纖佈線需求同步增加，帶動 MPO/Jumper、Fiber Harness、Shuffle Module 等產品需
求，台廠供應商包括光聖、波若威、佳必琪、貿聯-KY。
資料來源：福邦投顧彙整

### Page 11

11【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
光模組朝高速與高密度整合演進
• AI DC帶動高速光模組需求，2026年以400G、800G為主，1.6T開始放量；2027年1.6T出貨量
將明顯提升，並預期2028年導入3.2T。市調機構LightCounting預估，隨800G、1.6T等高速光
模組需求增加，矽光模組滲透率將提升，2026年矽光模組銷售額YoY成長110%，占整體光模
組市場約50%。
• 光模組速率提升是透過增加光通道或提高單通道速率來實現，以每通道200G為例，800G需4
個光通道，1.6T則增至8個，通道數增加所需的調變器、PD等元件數量也隨之增加，使耦合
及封裝複雜度提高，凸顯矽光模組的整合優勢
資料來源：LightCounting，千才，福邦投顧彙整

### Page 12

12【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
矽光PIC高度整合光元件並以半導體製程量產
• 矽光模組是指搭載矽光PIC的光模組，矽光PIC透過半導體製程，將調變器、波導等光學功能
整合於晶片，減少離散元件組裝及光學對準步驟，並有利於晶圓級量產。
• 2016年英特爾即推出矽光子光模組，惟早期高速傳輸需求有限，矽光PIC整合效益尚不顯著；
隨800G、1.6T以上高速光模組放量，通道數及光元件數量增加，矽光PIC整合優勢浮現。
• 現階段矽光模組仍沿用沿用OSFP、QSFP-DD既有可插拔光模組外型。
• 過去光通訊PIC多採III-V族材料，雷射與光元件可單晶整合，但市場規模較小且製程專用，多
以IDM方式生產；矽光PIC採CMOS相容製程，可導入成熟矽晶圓代工模式，實現規模量產與
降低成本，然無法與雷射整合。
資料來源：博通，福邦投顧彙整
圖7、傳統光模組與矽光子光模組架構比較

### Page 13

13【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
矽光PIC需求擴大，專業分工成形
• 矽光PIC晶圓代工目前以Tower半導體量產規模及客戶能見度最高；聯電亦積極切入，2025年
取得比利時半導體研究機構imec的12吋矽光子製程技術授權，2026年於新加坡12吋廠量產
1.6T PIC，預計2027年向更多客戶開放矽光代工平台。
• 除英特爾外，其餘具備矽光PIC設計能力業者並未單獨銷售PIC，而是將PIC與EIC、DSP整合
為光引擎(optical engine)後出售。
• 2023年英特爾出售光模組事業予Jabil，專注於提供整合雷射的矽光PIC。
資料來源：福邦投顧彙整

• Lumentum、Cohernet、博通採IDM模式，具備從LD設計、磊晶、晶圓製程至雷射封裝能力，

### Page 18

CPO發展趨勢與導入挑戰

### Page 19

19【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
CPO於3.2T世代有望加速滲透
• 台積電表示CPO模組將於2026年下半年量產，惟依供應鏈資訊，初期出貨量仍低、市場規模
有限。市調機構預估，CPO光模組出貨量將自2027年起明顯成長，2030年達約6,000萬顆；
滲透率部分，將由2027年的11%提升至2028年的19%，2030年進一步達38%。
• 業界普遍認為，傳輸速率進入3.2T世代後，CPO在功耗與頻寬密度上的優勢將更為明顯。博
通也表示，CPO的光互聯耗電量較可插拔光模組減少70%。
資料來源：Lightcounting，Dell’Oro, 福邦投顧彙整
圖10、CPO出貨量與滲透率預估

### Page 20

20【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
CPO推動光電共封裝，FAU成光耦合關鍵元件
• CPO為光電共封裝架構，將 PIC與EIC 整合為光引擎(OE)，再與Switch ASIC共封裝於同一載
板，目標降低電訊號傳輸距離與功耗。
• 雷射仍放置於交換器機箱前面板，且維持可插拔，以方便更換。
• 目前台積電以COUPE平台整合PIC與EIC成光引擎，然提高光耦合效率、降低光進入 PIC 的
損耗，成為CPO主要瓶頸之一，也使 FAU 成為關鍵元件。
資料來源：Photonics Spectra, 福邦投顧彙整

### Page 21

21【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
FAU難以晶片化，攸關CPO光耦合效率與量產時程
• 目前台積電COUPE平台優先採用光柵(grating)耦合架構，FAU以黏膠固定於光學引擎上
方。光線平行射出，需以反射鏡導入PIC；COUPE端利用矽透鏡將光聚焦至光柵耦合器
，也配置金屬反射鏡回收散射光，提升光耦合效率。
• 光耦合效率攸關光學元件設計與光纖精密對位。FAU除涉及光學設計外，光纖亦需精準
置入V-groove，並完成固定、端面研磨與對位封裝，工序繁雜而被視為CPO規模量產瓶
頸之一。
• FAU投入業者主要包括線材廠、傳統FAU業者及光學元件廠。例如，光聖旗下合聖推出
以Metalens搭配反射鏡的可拆卸式FAU；大立光則推出配置80顆微型鏡頭、整合反射鏡
與稜鏡功能的PMLA，並進一步布局多層堆疊光纖。線材廠方面，以波若威、佳必琪與
貿聯為代表；傳統FAU業者則以上銓為代表。
資料來源：TSMC, 福邦投顧彙整
圖12、TSMC COUPE的FAU光耦合架構
大立光、合聖針對反射鏡推出解決方案

### Page 22

22【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
FAU技術升級聚焦提升耦光效率與縮短對位時間
• FAU主要發展方向為可拆卸設計、多層光纖堆疊與被動對位。其中，可拆卸式方案以美、日
業者布局較積極，主要考量FAU或光纖生損壞可直接更換，避免拆除整組光學引擎，降低維
修難度及交換器損壞風險。
• 目前FAU多採主動對位，透過反覆微調位置並量測光功率，找出最佳耦合點後固定，雖可補
償製造與組裝誤差，但組裝時間長。被動對位仰賴高精度製程與定位結構，將精度要求前移
，以簡化後段組裝並提升量產效率
資料來源：各業者, 福邦投顧彙整
圖13、FAU正發展可拆卸、多層堆疊及被動對準
日商Senko的可拆卸FAU
Teramount雙層光纖設計使
光纖量從單層的32條增為
64條。
Teramount與高塔半導體合作開發Bump-ready wafer，
即在PIC晶圓階段預先製作光學耦合與對位結構。

### Page 23

23【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
NPO持續演進 成為Scale Up另一選擇
• NPO被視為CPO折衷方案，光引擎置於鄰近交換器ASIC，降低傳輸損耗且保留維修彈性。
• 華為認為NPO具備提供400G/lane的潛力，3.2T世代未必需全面導入CPO。
• 博通、Marvell、Coherent皆已展示或推出NPO方案，光源架構部分，Marvell主打無DSP、配
置CW雷射；博通展示VCSEL方案，Coherent同時推出VCSEL和CW方案，VCSEL功耗低，
晶片尺寸小，可高密度排列，並與光引擎整合封裝，然傳輸距離有限，適合Scale-up應用。
• 被視為Marvell合作業者的華星光，預期2027年下半開始量產1.6T NPO模組，2028年出貨量
體將較為顯著。
資料來源：華為, 福邦投顧彙整
圖14、NPO與CPO技術路徑比較

### Page 24

24【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
XPO重塑可插拔模組 挑戰CPO的Scale Out市場
• 美網通業者Arista於2026年成立XPO MSA，並展示首代支援200G/lane，頻寬12.8T的可插拔
XPO模組，後續將發展400G/lane、單模組頻寬25.6T，惟量產與規模商用時程尚未明朗。
XPO MSA成員橫跨CSP、ASIC、光元件與光模組供應鏈，包括微軟、Marvell、Coherent、
Lumentum及新易盛等。Arista為Ethernet交換器設備商，主導XPO規格與生態系發展。
• XPO主打Scale-out應用，強調單一XPO模組可取代8顆OSFP，在有限機箱空間內提高整體傳
輸頻寬。由於XPO採全新模組機構，交換器須配合重新設計供電、散熱及相關介面，目前規
格制定與系統平台仍以Arista為主；儘管XPO MSA讓CSP在光模組供應商選擇上更具彈性，
但在其他交換器業者尚未跟進前，導入XPO亦可能意味對Arista交換器平台的更高依賴。
資料來源：福邦投顧彙整

### Page 25

25【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
Open CPX以標準化介面挑戰NVIDIA封閉式CPO架構
• 美系光傳輸設備商Ciena與Coherent，以及連接器業者Molex、Samtec推動Open CPX MSA，
透過標準介面讓讓光引擎可拆卸，獨立於ASIC，提高光引擎與ASIC的選擇彈性。
• Open CPX強調架構選擇彈性，CSP可依系統需求採用NPO或CPO，雷射可選外部雷射或整合
式雷射方案。
• Ciena已推出採Open CPX架構的光引擎Vesta，，並以16 顆6.4T光引擎打造總頻寬102.4T的系
統。Ciena於3Q26法說表示，Vesta樣品已售出，預計2027年開始貢獻營收、2028年放量。
資料來源：Ciena，福邦投顧彙整
圖16、Open CPX採可拆卸光引擎設計，提升供應商選擇彈性
強調光模組配置支援CPX
規格的連接器，實現可拆卸OpenCPX成員可見到連接器廠商如
Molex 和Samtec。

### Page 26

26【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
各種光互聯架構重塑供應鏈價值分配
資料來源：福邦投顧彙整
受惠供應鏈 承壓供應鏈
CPO/NPO 光引擎、外部雷射、FAU 傳統可插拔光模組
XPO 可插拔光模組、雷射 相對有限
Open CPX 可拆卸光引擎、連接器、雷射、FAU 封閉式CPO供應鏈
• CPO和NPO的供應鏈差異有限，皆由交換器ASIC或加速卡業者主導系統架構，光引擎、雷
射、FAU、連接器等供應鏈皆配合設計。若CPO滲透率提高，將壓縮傳統可插拔光模組需求
，但具雷射組裝能力的業者仍可供應外部雷射
• CPO的光耦合效率需求提高，使FAU重要性提升。
• XPO藉提高模組頻寬，延續可插拔方案在高速傳輸的生命週期。
• Open CPX採用標準介面實現光引擎可拆卸，由於PCB板端需配置CPX連接器，使連接器重
要性提升。
• 不論採用何種光互連架構，隨AI資料中心傳輸頻寬與光路數持續增加，高密度佈線需求均將
提升，帶動fiber jumper、fiber harness及fiber shuffle需求，如光聖已切入Google資料中心光纖

連接供應鏈將明顯受惠。
表5、不同光互連架構受惠與承壓供應鏈比較

### Page 27

27【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
CPO長期具導入需求，惟時程仍具不確定性
• CPO在Scale-out面臨傳統可插拔光模組、XPO與NPO等架構競爭；相較下，台積電認為CPO
在Scale-up更能發揮高頻寬、低功耗與短距離互連優勢，朝Optical I/O發展。
• Scale-up 架構目前存在技術路線分歧—Narrow & Fast 與 Wide & Slow；前者持續提高單通道
速率，以NVIDIA為代表，後者增加道數來擴充頻寬，微軟MOSAIC為較典型方案。
• 展望未來，儘管CPO導入時程受系統架構與成本效益影響，但隨運算規模持續擴大，銅互連
瓶頸將推動光引擎進一步靠近交換器ASIC或XPU，CPO長期導入趨勢不變。 再者，NVIDIA
亦透過NVLink Fusion持續擴大Fabric生態系，使採用客製XPU的業者仍可導入NVLink架構
，擴大CPO潛在應用市場。
資料來源：TSMC, 福邦投顧彙整

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
• Any forecast rests on assumptions. Each might be
individually reasonable, but if they all tilt in the same
direction, they look less plausible in combination.
• Different assumptions can also drive different conclusions,
as can be seen in Treasury and RBA views on trend growth

in supply capacity. Treasury’s long-term view on both
productivity growth and labour force participation trends is
systematically less downbeat than the RBA’s (shorter-term)
view. Treasury’s estimate of the stable-inflation rate of
unemployment is also lower than the RBA’s estimate.
• We consider the risks around the RBA’s view to be
asymmetric. The RBA’s assumptions implicitly require a
repeat of the factors that dragged recent productivity
outcomes down, despite strong global investment, policy

changes domestically and the possible benefits of AI. Some
observers appear to extrapolate this weakness beyond the
RBA’s forecast horizon. But this would be hard to square
with the reasonable belief that the global interest rate
structure will be higher in future than it was pre-pandemic.
A systematic tilt, even if unintentional and understandable,
may point to the likely direction of future surprises.
Any forecast rests on assumptions, and the further out

you forecast, the harder it is to ground those assumptions
in something real. Add the uncertainty about AI, where
predictions range from “unbounded prosperity without
needing to work” to “we will all be dead in a couple of years”,
and you can see why assuming the future looks like the past is
an attractive fall-back position.
How much of the past, though? This is the context for the
debate about the Intergenerational Review (IGR)  released this
week. In one corner, Treasury assumes that future productivity

growth will average 1.2%yr over the next 40 years, in line with
the 30-year average, and that labour force participation will
continue to trend up, as it has for the past five decades, until
2040. In the other corner, the RBA assumes that productivity
growth will average 0.7%yr, in line with the past 20 years
– essentially the post-GFC and pandemic period – and that
participation rates will be flat to down in the near term.
Both productivity assumptions have limitations: round-number-

of-year averages might be distorted by capturing a downturn
without the matching upturn, or vice versa. Peak-to-peak or
trough-to-trough is always preferable. Even that calculation
cannot escape the weakness of recent years, though.
When your assumptions have only upside risk
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
To be fair, the RBA’s productivity assumptions relate only to
the short term and are not strictly comparable to the IGR,
as the Governor has emphasised this week.  By contrast, the

near-term divergence in forecasts for the participation rate,
which my colleague Ryan Wells  first highlighted in May , is a
genuine divergence.
The RBA’s more downbeat set of assumptions could be
unwittingly contributing to a self-fulfilling talking-down
of Australia’s prospects that clearly appeals to some
commentators outside the public sector. Many in this group
view productivity growth as stemming wholly or mostly from
policy “reforms”, harking back to the halcyon Hawke–Keating

era of deregulation. Proponents point to the lack of reform
recently to justify this downbeat view of growth capacity. We
think this misses the global nature of that slowdown.
Part of the trouble with the downbeat view of future
productivity is that it implicitly assumes that the factors
weighing on productivity growth in the past will continue.
But there are good reasons not to expect the same post-GFC
weakness in risk appetite, and so investment and capital

accumulation, decades later and absent another crisis. The AI
boom and associated resurgence of risk appetite make it hard
to argue that we will see the same lack of new productivity-
enhancing technologies and general malaise as that period.
For Australia specifically, assuming the same low average
productivity growth as the past 20 years would require
assuming a compositional drag s imilar to a repeat of the
run-up of the care economy and decline in measured mining

production over the past 15 years. To be fair, decarbonisation
does imply further declines in (capital-intensive, high-
productivity) coal production, dragging mining productivity
down further. It would be a stretch, though, to assume
expansion in critical and transition minerals provides no offset.
We can grant that AI’s impact on productivity will take a while
to come through and still expect some boost in coming years
– at least by the end of the RBA’s forecast horizon in 2028.

Indeed, some observers already see an AI-induced boost to
service-sector activity in the recent upside surprise in UK GDP.
Some productivity pessimists might want to argue that the
“RBA assumptions collectively imply a
downbeat view of supply growth."

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
rest of the world will benefit from the AI boom, but somehow
not Australia, or not until later. Such an extraordinary claim
requires a mechanism explaining that divergence, though.
The Productivity Commission estimated back in December
that AI could boost multifactor productivity by 2.3%pt over

the course of the next decade, or roughly a quarter-point per
year extra growth on average – a big difference in the scheme
of usual growth rates in output and productivity. Labour
productivity growth would be boosted a little more; the data
centre boom is expanding the capital stock. But an estimate
published in December is unlikely to adequately capture the
step change in capability of the main models, especially the
coding and agentic harnesses, that emerged over the summer

of 2025/26. Given what we understand to be limited adoption
of AI in the Australian public sector, policymakers might also
be systematically underestimating both the technology’s
capabilities and the breadth of uptake in the private sector.
As an illustration of how downbeat you need to be to
extrapolate the RBA’s near-term assumption further out,
suppose that all AI does is make about 10% of the economy
10% more productive over four years. This extra 1% of

economy-wide productivity is well below most estimates
of the impact. It would nonetheless add a quarter-point on
annual productivity growth for four years, bringing a downbeat
0.7% assumption up to almost 1%.
A deeper challenge is that, beyond the RBA’s near-term
horizon, low productivity growth is hard to square with a
higher future average for interest rates globally.
We note, for example, recent comments by RBA Deputy
Governor Hauser, pointing to 700 years of bond yield data

to suggest that the post-GFC period of low interest rates
was an anomaly, something we noted two years ago. The
global structure of interest rates emerges to balance global
saving and investment, and stronger investment for AI and
other purposes is clearly in evidence. We find it implausible
to assume that all that investment happens, pushing up
global yields, and none of it delivers higher productivity. Even
increased defence spending has historically had productivity

payoffs, as Ukraine’s drone manufacturing ecosystem has
exemplified recently.
It is understandable that when inflation is above target, a
central bank would rather err on the side of caution than
techno-optimism. They know that being downbeat, and
running policy a little too tight, means a faster return of
inflation to target, which is no bad thing for an inflation-
targeting central bank.
It matters, though, when those understandable assumptions

are systematically one-sided. If there are arguments that
productivity growth over the next few years will be even lower
than the 0.7%yr 20-year average, we are yet to see them. In
contrast, there are reasonable arguments for it to be a bit higher.
Similarly, the RBA’s participation rate assumptions are low
given near-term demographic trends , and out of step with
Treasury’s revised projections. Today’s labour market data
for August shows the participation rate rising again, to just

12½ basis points below the January 2025 all-time high. This
supports the idea that RBA’s view on participation trends
is too downbeat. The IGR’s NAIRU (stable-inflation rate of
unemployment) assumption is a quarter-point or so below
RBA estimates revealed in various FOI requests and the
Governor’s comments this week.
Together, the three RBA assumptions imply weaker growth
in supply capacity and thus more inflationary pressure for any
given level of demand. If every one of your assumptions seems

individually plausible, but all skew in the one direction, it
starts to look like a pattern. The thumb on the scale is unlikely
to be intentional, but it does suggest the likely direction of
future surprises in the medium term.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: the importance of supply
This week, market participants have focused on discussions
between the US and China on trade, security and technology
as President Xi visits Washington.
Preliminary discussions between US Treasury Secretary

Bessent and Chinese Vice Premier He Lifeng ahead of
President Xi’s arrival in Washington were characterised by
both sides as productive, and Treasury Secretary Bessent
subsequently announced that discussions in Washington
would leave trade negotiations for upcoming meetings
between the two leaders in Shenzhen and Miami. To allow
room for this deferral, the current trade truce will be extended
until January 2027. President Trump subsequently outlined

that discussions during President Xi’s Washington visit would
instead focus on security, technology and AI.
A more constructive tone was also seen between the US and
Iran overnight, if press reports are accurate. According to
Bloomberg and Reuters, sources suggest the US and Iran are
again exploring a staged deal which would see Iran re-open
the Strait of Hormuz and the US lift its blockade of Iranian
ports. Negotiations are occurring on the sidelines of the UN

General Assembly, with Qatari officials mediating.
President Trump continues to show little interest in a deal
before November’s mid-term elections, but the global
economy is under increasing pressure as the price of Brent oil
hovers between USD100 and USD110 while refining spreads
add additional impetus to refined product prices. The mooted
consideration of a potential US diesel export ban this week
makes clear that the US is not immune from this pressure

despite having significant domestic energy supply and refining
capacity, relative to their own demand.
The consequences of the current energy supply shock and other
disruptions the US have experienced over the past year remain
front of mind for FOMC members. Those speaking this week such
as NY and Cleveland Fed Presidents Williams and Hammack
highlighted the risk to inflation expectations from a series of
supply shocks when the economy is fully employed, and the

likelihood of an additional hike(s) being required to rein in current
inflation momentum and risks. Williams noted that expectations
of another hike by year end are "reasonable" and that the FOMC
have "a lot of work to do". Debt market’s concern remains acute,
having now almost priced in four hikes over the coming year and
taken the US 10-year above 5.00%, currently 5.20%.
Australia’s only data release this week was the August
Labour Force Survey . Employment surprised to the upside,

rising 39.5k. But, once again, new labour demand was more
than offset by supply as the participation rate bounced
0.2ppts higher to 67.1%, just shy of January 2025’s 115-year
high of 67.2%. As a result, the unemployment rate increased
from 4.5% to 4.6% and was close to rounding up to 4.7%
(4.65% to two decimal places).
There may be some residual seasonality in August’s print
related to the new survey collection model. However, the
underlying trend is still consistent with a gradual easing in the

labour market, seeing the unemployment rate drift higher. We
expect this to continue through to the first half of 2027.
The strength in labour force participation also speaks to the
broader issue of Australia’s supply capacity. As discussed
by Chief Economist Luci Ellis , the RBA’s assumptions around
participation, productivity and the NAIRU collectively
skew towards a weak assessment of supply capacity. For
participation in particular, the RBA’s near-term assumptions

are downbeat compared to recent demographic trends and
also sit below Treasury’s longer-term projections as set out in
the 2026 IGR. As above, the August LFS emphasises that slack
could emerge more quickly than the RBA’s forecasts imply
without an outright fall in employment.
Ahead of next week’s RBA meeting and inflation data,
our latest state-level analysis  provides perspective on the
persistence of underlying price pressures, particularly the

importance of housing-related components. For a broader
assessment of the fiscal backdrop and the implications for the
states, our latest state fiscal update  explores how the housing
market correction and changes to NDIS funding arrangements
are pressuring state finances.
Before turning to this week’s events offshore, it is important
to highlight that last Friday the Bank of Japan raised its
policy rate by 25bp to 1.25%. Policymakers maintained a

constructive assessment of the economy, pointing to resilient
consumption, ongoing labour market tightness, and solid
investment spending. More importantly, the Bank expressed
growing confidence that inflation is increasingly being
sustained by domestic wage and demand dynamics rather
than imported cost shocks alone.
In our view, the emphasis placed on improving underlying
inflation dynamics suggests policymakers are becoming
increasingly comfortable that inflation is able to be sustained by

domestic demand and wage growth. Normalisation is likely to
remain gradual, but the Bank appears firmly on a path towards
further tightening, with rates likely to reach 1.5% by mid-2027.
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist

### Page 5

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
5
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
Along for the ride
There was plenty of action in the markets this week, as global
interest rates continued to move higher, and New Zealand
was taken along for the ride. In the US, strong economic data
and resurgent oil prices have led the market to believe that

the central bank will need to get on with the job and hike
its policy rate further in the months ahead. Longer-term US
interest rates have continued to lift sharply, driving both a rise
in domestic interest rates and a fall in the New Zealand dollar,
with the trade-weighted index already 4% below what the
RBNZ assumed in its September Monetary Policy Statement.
While the September MPS worked to dampen market
expectations about the pace of future rate hikes, offshore

developments have seen market pricing for the OCR bounce back
and exceed previous levels. Interest rate markets are currently
pricing around a 75% chance of a hike at the 28 October policy
review, with a high chance of another hike on top of this at the 9
December review. We’d question whether the RBNZ’s thinking
has really shifted to that degree in the last few weeks though.
Notably, the RBNZ provided some unscheduled comments on
the monetary policy outlook this week, in what was otherwise

a very light local news week. In connection with a visit to
Dunedin by Governor Breman and other RBNZ leaders, the
RBNZ put out a media release that included the following:
“If higher oil prices persist, they are expected to result in
somewhat higher near-term inflation than we assumed in the
September Statement… As always, we will assess incoming
data and global developments ahead of our next decision in
October and remain focused on the outlook for inflation over

the medium term.”
Both of these statements are fairly benign and factual in
themselves. What’s more notable is that the RBNZ felt the
need to make a public comment at all; the occasion didn’t
require one. (The Governor’s presentation at a business lunch
was originally billed as being based on material from the
September MPS, with no new insights.)
If the intention was to guide market expectations for the
October meeting lower, it wasn’t entirely successful. Indeed,

the market initially fixated on the first part of the statement –
higher near-term inflation – and pushed pricing for an October
hike even higher on the day. While this was unwound the
following day, market pricing for OCR hikes remains higher
than it was at the start of the week.
As it stands, that leaves the RBNZ with the prospect of
rattling the market if it follows through with the October
pause that it signalled in September MPS. However, it’s

possible that the local and offshore economic data, together
with geopolitical developments, could shift market pricing in
the meantime. Domestically, the NZIER’s quarterly survey of
business opinion (6 Oct) and the September quarter CPI (22
Oct) will be especially important in that regard.
Turning to the data, the only release this week was our
Employment Confidence Index, produced in conjunction with
McDermott Miller. Compared to our consumer confidence

survey, there was a relatively weak bounce in employment
confidence in the September quarter. The index rose by 3.2
points to 86.3, leaving it at the second-lowest level on record
for this survey going back to 2004.
Perceptions of current job opportunities were unchanged at a
net -60%. This is the component of the survey that seems to
have the most predictive power for near-term labour market
conditions and is broadly consistent with the unemployment

rate holding at its current 11-year high of 5.6%. Hiring has
remained soft to an unusual degree for this stage of the
economic cycle, and those who are out of the workforce have
been finding it increasingly difficult to get in.
It’s clear that there is still substantial slack in the labour
market and little upward pressure on wages, which would
push back somewhat against the market’s view that the RBNZ
will need to pick up the pace and deliver two more OCR hikes

before Christmas. The RBNZ doesn’t need to have employment
formally in its mandate for it to be attentive to the signals
coming from the labour market.
The data flow picks up again next week, with monthly releases
for employment, building activity, and business and consumer
confidence – see our previews on the following page. The
other release of note is the Pre-Election Fiscal and Economic
Update (PREFU) on Tuesday. This ‘book opening’ exercise
is designed to create a level playing field, allowing parties

outside the current Government and the public more generally
to judge to the state of the public finances in the leadup to the
General Election on 7 November. In the case of the opposition
parties, it will also allow them to calibrate the spending and
taxation policies on which they will campaign. See our preview
for the details on what we expect from this release.
Michael Gordon
Senior Economist

AUS: Aug Household Spending Indicator (%mth)
Sep 29, Last: 1.1%, WBC f/c: –0.5%
Mkt f/c: 0.3%, Range: –0.5% to 0.7%
The household spending indicator rose 1.1%mth in July,
comfortably exceeding expectations and extending the strong
momentum evident through the middle of the year. Annual

growth accelerated to 7.0%yr, the fastest pace since early
2023.
Our Westpac-DataX Card Tracker points to softer spending
conditions through August and early September, with higher
fuel prices increasingly driving spending growth and, at the
margin, squeezing spending elsewhere. The detail suggests
a modest rotation away from discretionary categories, with
hospitality spending easing and vehicle sales also likely
moderating after a strong June quarter. Together with a softer

read from our MHSI-aligned card measure, we anticipate the
household spending indicator will decline 0.5%mth in August.
AUS: RBA Policy Decision (%)
Sep 29, Last: 4.35, Westpac f/c: 4.60
Market f/c: 4.60, Range: 4.60
Westpac anticipates the RBA Monetary Policy Board will raise
the cash rate by 25bps to 4.60% at its September meeting.
As discussed by Chief Economist Luci Ellis recent rhetoric from
the RBA have escalated, particularly with Governor Bullock

flagging that upside inflation risks appear to be "materialising"
– the conditionality set in August for further rate hikes.
There is a risk of a follow-up hike, though it crucially depends
on the data flow after the meeting. For September, we
continue to expect a split vote at the meeting, with material
differences in opinion about trend growth in supply capacity,
as discussed on Page 2.
AUS: Aug Headline CPI (%mth)
Sep 30, Last: 1.0, Westpac f/c: 0.4

Market f/c: 0.5, Range: 0.4 to 0.9
The July CPI reversed May and June weakness with a larger
than expected 1.0% increase. This was stronger than
Westpac’s expectation of 0.8% and market’s expectation of
0.9%. The annual pace eased to 3.5%yr from 3.8%yr.
We are looking for a 0.4% increase in the August CPI. Food &
non-alcoholic beverages to rise 0.2%mth with solid gains for
bread & cereal products (0.4%), meat & seafood (0.5%) and
food products n.e.c. (0.4%) partially offset by falling non-

alcoholic beverages (–0.6%) and a soft print for meals out &
take away food (0.1%).
At two decimal points it is a 0.43% increase in August so the
risk this month is to the upside.
For more details please see “ August CPI Preview ”.

AUS: Aug Trimmed Mean CPI (%mth)
Sep 30, Last: 0.5, Westpac f/c: 0.2
Market f/c: 0.3, Range: 0.2 to 0.4
The Trimmed Mean (TM) lifted 0.5%mth/3.5%yr, on par with
last July which was the strongest monthly read in the series’
short history. The result was stronger than both our and the

market’s expectation of 0.4%mth.
August TM is expected to rise 0.2%mth lifting the annual pace
to 3.6%yr. For market services excluding volatiles and holiday
travel we have pencilled in a step down to 0.1%mth, this series
lifted 0.8% in July possibly due to the larger-than-expected
increase in minimum wages.
The distribution of the August TM is from a low of –0.32%mth
to a high of 0.53%mth. Remembering the TM is seasonally
adjusted, with rents (+0.17%mth) and dwellings (+0.20%mth)

mostly locking in the TM. See “ August CPI Preview ”.
AUS: Aug Dwelling Approvals (%mth)
Sep 30, Last: –3.6, Westpac f/c: –5.0
Market f/c: –1.0, Range: –6.0 to 1.0
Dwelling approvals fell 3.6%mth in July, with annual growth
easing to 9.0%yr. The decline was driven primarily by detached
dwellings, although unit approvals also edged lower. The
result suggests higher interest rates may be starting to weigh
on new housing demand.
We expect a similar story to play out in August, with

elevated interest rates continuing to weigh on detached
dwelling approvals, while the unit segment holds broadly
steady. Overall, dwelling approvals are expected to decline
5%mth. While risks remain tilted to the downside, residential
construction should remain relatively resilient through 2026,
supported by the strong lift in approvals through the first half
of the year and a sizeable pipeline of work yet to be done.
AUS: Aug Private Sector Credit (%mth)

Sep 30, Last: 0.6, Westpac f/c: 0.5
Market f/c: 0.5, Range: 0.3 to 0.6
Growth in private sector credit growth appeared unsustainable
over May-Jun, especially given the slowing in the housing
market. Come July, the pace now dropped from 0.8%mth to
0.6%mth. Housing credit the main source of weakness, with
investor credit growth beginning to adjust lower in response to
higher interest rates, lower house prices and, most importantly,
tax changes for investment properties. Meanwhile, business

borrowing continued to rise rapidly.
For August, we have pencilled in a 0.5%mth increase, and we
look for continued divergence between housing and business
segments.

### Page 8

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.8
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Sep Cotality Home Value Index (%mth)
Oct 1, Last: –0.9, Westpac f/c: –0.9
The Cotality home value index – covering all of Australia – fell
0.9% in August following downwardly-revised 1.2% and 0.9%
declines in July and June. Annual growth slowed to 2.7%yr.

Prices nationally are now 3.6% below their peak in March.
The September update is set to see more of the same with
the daily measure (covering the five major capital cities rather
than all of Australia) tracking a 1.2% decline in line with
this measure’s August move. Annual growth looks like to
stall about flat. The detail suggests monthly declines have
accelerated in Brisbane, Perth and Adelaide but moderated a
touch in Melbourne, noting that there is a seasonal element

to price moves in Sep. Markets are still adjusting to higher
interest rates and the tax policy changes introduced in the May
Federal budget with more weakness likely into year-end.
AUS: Aug Goods Trade Balance ($bn)
Oct 1, Last: 1.9, Westpac f/c: 2.3
Market f/c: 2.2, Range: 1.5 to 2.5
The goods trade surplus narrowed slightly to $1.9bn in July
but remained above our and consensus expectations. This
was down from an upwardly revised surplus of $2.3bn in June.

Underneath the modest headline change was considerable
volatility among components, especially in flows of non-
monetary gold, remain the chief culprit.
For August, we have pencilled in a slight widening of the trade
surplus to $2.3bn. Port data points to a bounce-back in coal
and iron ore export volumes,  and prices looked to have edged
higher. However, volatility in non-monetary gold trade and
other hard-to-predict sub-categories (like ADP equipment)

remain a wildcard.
NZ: Aug Monthly Employment Indicator (%mth)
Sep 28, Last: 0.3, Westpac f/c: 0.3
The MEI has continued to tick higher in recent months,
showing that the jobs market has held its ground through
the Iran conflict and the consequent surge in fuel prices. The
weekly snapshots provided by Stats NZ have been running
ahead of year-ago levels and suggest another solid result
for August – we’ve pencilled in a 0.3% increase, following
a similar rise in July. However, the history of this indicator

suggests that both months are likely to be revised down a bit
from their initial release.

### Page 9

Nonfarm payrolls surprised to the upside in August, 
registering a gain of 162k with 55k in back revisions to June 
and July. The report left the 3-month average at 71k, near the 
top of the estimated range consitent with balance between 
labour demand and supply. Come September, the trend is 
expected to remain in place with a 80k gain forecast. With 
inflation risks paramount, upward revisions will have a greater 
impact on market variables than a negative revision.
The decline in the participation rate over the past year has 
capped the unemployment rate. Participation is likely to edge 
higher in September and take the unemployment rate up 
to 4.2% with it. There is little available slack in the labour 
market, but plenty just outside if demand lifts.

## Document 233: JPM_Taiwan_Cooling_Compo_2026-09-29_5458761 NV VRU AWS GOOGLE

Source PDF: all report pdf/JPM_Taiwan_Cooling_Compo_2026-09-29_5458761 NV VRU AWS GOOGLE.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 15
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

J.P. Morgan Securities (Asia Pacific) Limited/ J.P.
Morgan Broking (Hong Kong) Limited
Albert Hung
(886-2) 2725-9875
albert.hung@jpmchase.com
J.P. Morgan Securities (Taiwan) Limited/ J.P.
Morgan Securities (Asia Pacific) Limited/ J.P.
Morgan Broking (Hong Kong) Limited
We see an accelerating cooling spec upgrade trend in both AI ASICs and GPU
systems, and the content uplift looks larger than investors’ expectations, especially
for Nvidia’s next-gen VR Ultra (VRU), contrary to recent market concerns on

potential de-specing. We continue to believe that cooling is the key bottleneck to
unlocking continued TDP growth of all AI chips and migration of AI server
systems. For VRU, we see new designs and spec upgrades across cold plate
(double-sided design and diamond copper material adoption) , quick
disconnect (MQD upgrading to ZQD),  and lid/heat spreader (3-piece/
removable lid design). We believe the stronger-than-expected content growth in
Nvidia VRU, along with the further increasing ASIC and AMD GPU cooling value

will continue to benefit the leading suppliers of each component. Looking forward
to the next 1-2 years, we believe the CPO/NPO switches and higher-spec optical
transceivers/modules will become the next driver with mainstream adoption of
liquid cooling in future generations (likely from 1.6T/3.2T gens). We prefer A VC
(OW) and Fositek (OW) in the cooling space.
• Cold plate: double-sided design likely adopted in Nvidia VRU, AWS Trn3
and AMD MI450 to drive +50% content; diamond copper to add value.

The double-sided cold plate design looks set to become the next step-up in
cooling specs. We expect adoption in AWS Trainium 3 (Trn3) and AMD
MI450 compute trays, with Nvidia VRU likely next. This new double-sided
design was required given the VPD (Vertical Power Delivery) architecture,
which moves the power discretes, SOCAMM, and capacitors to the backside
of chip board, requiring incremental cold plates for backside cooling. VRU
Bianca cold plates may also upgrade their material to “diamond copper” for

better cooling effectiveness, which adds further value per unit. This
combination supports meaningful cooling content upside for VRU (~50%
increase vs. VR200) versus market expectations for flat-to-down cooling
content on de-spec concerns. Outside these platforms, Google TPU v8 should
drive solid cooling content via gold-plated cold plates plus additional units
adopted for surrounding ICs. TPU v9’s larger chip die and higher TDP also
increase the odds of the double-sided cold plate adoption.

• Quick disconnect (QD): No content change with the potential VRU 9-
switch tray design of 1.5U with “double-layer architecture”; meaningful
upgrades in VRU (ZQD adoption) with ~100% content increase per rack.
In the VRU system, we believe Nvidia will upgrade some of its QDs from the
previous MQD (Multi Quick Disconnect)/UQD (Universal Quick Disconnect)
to the higher-spec ZQD (Zero-drip/flow rate Quick Disconnect) with ~2x ASP,
in order to help improve the cooling efficiency with better liquid flow rate and

limited leakage. Per JPMe, the total QD content per VRU NVL72 rack will
increase by ~100% vs VR200/GB300 racks, as the ZQD with 2x ASP will be
adopted in 1) the compute tray Bianca modules (upgrading to ZQD06 spec
along with GPU cold plate upgrades), 2) entry QDs for all 9 compute trays and
9 “double-layered” switch trays (the 2 out-of-tray plug QDs upgrading to
ZQD14 spec), and 3) rack manifold socket QDs (upgrading to ZQD14 as well).
• Heat spreader: No delay of 3-piece/removable lid adoption in Rubin Ultra,

Investment thesis
We believe the cooling component sector will continue to outperform the market, and in
this space, we prefer A VC and Fositek. Compared to other cooling names, these two
names 1) still trade at an undemanding valuation (vs historical) with near-term share
price performance underperforming other cooling names (or even Taiex for A VC), 2)

can continue to deliver both near-term and longer-term earnings upside (e.g. 3Q margin
beat, 2028/28E EPS upside from more meaningful spec upgrades), and 3) serve as the
leading players in the specific components, enabling the major beneficiaries of spec
upgrades and ongoing margin expansion (with smaller-than-expected ASP pressure).

Compute tray level: cooling content to increase another 30-50% in VRU driven by
double-sided cold plate adding diamond copper materials and ZQD adoption

• Cold plate: We believe the cooling content (including cold plate, QD and inner
manifold) of VRU compute tray can reach US$3,000-3,500 level, which suggests
30-50% content increase vs current VR design, and should be better than market
expectation of limited changes or even de-spec. The stronger content growth in
VRU compute tray is mainly driven by 1) the double-sided cold plate design for
Bianca modules given VPD (vertical power delivery) adoption (placed at the

backside of chip): making it 6 cold plates (2 existing + 4 at the backside for rack
GPU) on the upper half of tray for the two Bianca modules (vs 2 CP units in VR
upper-half tray) + the existing 3 cold plates at the bottom half for the CX-9 chips
and power distribution board (PDB), 2) diamond copper material adoption:
specifically added for the cold plate areas attaching on HBMs, 3) additional QDs
required (additional 4 sets/8 units of Mini QD, in our view) for the additional cold

plates (the ones for the VPD placed at the backside of chip), and 4) QDs for the
Bianca module cold plates upgrading to ZQD from MQD previously with ~2x
ASP increase.
• QD: In VRU system, we believe Nvidia will upgrade some of its QDs from the
previous MQD (Multi Quick Disconnect)/UQD (Universal Quick Disconnect) to the
higher-spec ZQD (Zero-drip/flow rate Quick Disconnect) to help improve the
cooling efficiency with better liquid flow rate and limited leakage. Hence, we

believe the VRU compute tray QD unit will be largely the same as VR with 20

Switch tray level: 2x QD unit adoption per VRU switch tray (1.5U) with the

• Cold plate: Given the new design of “2 layers of boards per 1.5U switch tray (2

switch boards each at 0.75U)”, we model the cooling content (including cold plate,
QD and inner manifold) of the 1.5U switch tray to increase to at least US$3k, based
on 2x cold plate module adopted given 2 switch boards.
• QD: The QD units inside the “1.5U” VRU switch tray will likely double vs that in
VR, implying an increase to (8+2) units from (4+2) units, given 2 layers/boards per
tray. Although the 8 units of QD inside the VRU switch tray should all remain with

the MQD06 spec, the 2x unit increase still drives overall QD content growth. In
addition, the 2 QDs outside of VRU switch tray will be upgraded to ZQD14 from
UQD08 in VR, which also adds incremental content from ASP increase for these 2
plug QDs. In total, we model the QD content per switch tray to increase to ~US$260
for VRU vs ~US$150 for VR, based on our estimates.

• Cold plate: We believe the cooling content (including cold plate, QD and inner
manifold) of AWS Trn3 compute tray can reach US$3,000-3,500 level (~30% higher
than Nvidia’s VR compute tray), thanks mainly to the double-sided cold plate
design (8 CPs for 4 ASICs) plus 1 incremental tray-sized cold plate for the

baseboard with decent amount of QD adoption. Also, the Trn3 switch tray may also
create US$1,500-2,000 content value, with slightly more trays per rack (10 switch
trays in Teton 3 Max vs 9 units in VR NVL72 rack). Therefore, the total cooling
content (including cold plate, QD and inner manifold) per Teton 3 Max rack
could reach ~US$86k (vs <US$70k content for VR NVL72 rack).
• QD: AWS Teton 3 Max adopts UQD and MQD in their Trn3 compute and switch

tray designs, and the total QD unit adoption per Teton 3 Max rack is 512 units vs
450 units in Nvidia VR NVL72 rack (both excluding rack manifold socket QDs).
Each QD ASP is at ~US$20, so the total QD content per AWS Teton 3 Max (Trn3
liquid-cooled rack) is ~US$10.2k per NRL72 rack.

• Cold plate: We believe the cooling content (including cold plate, QD and inner
manifold) of TPU v8 compute tray can reach US$2-2.5k level, similar to Nvidia’s
VR compute tray despite lower chip TDP, due to the more complicated cold plate
design, including 1) more cold plate unit adoption: a total of 12 cold plates with 4

big ones for TPUs and an additional 8 small ones for surrounding chips, and 2) gold-
plating design (given metal TIM adoption) to add incremental content. Therefore,
the total cooling content (including cold plate, QD and inner manifold) per TPU
v8 rack could reach ~US$38k.
• QD: Google TPUv8 compute tray adopts NSP1 QD, unlike Nvidia or other CSP
ASIC systems that generally use MQD or UQD, and it only places 2 units of NSP1
QD per TPU compute tray (vs 22 units in VR and Trn3 compute tray). In terms of

ASP, we believe NSP1 QD is 2-3x higher than both MQD and UQD. However,
based on our calculation, the total QD content of Google TPU is relatively small
at ~US$1.8k with only 32 units per rack (vs 400 or 500 unit level for VR and Trn3
rack), despite higher ASP.
• Heat spreader: Google TPUs also adopt stiffeners for their heat spreader design,
including both v8i and v8t. However, for TPU v9, we believe the removable lid +
stiffener could be a potential solution, given much bigger size and higher TDP

(potentially reaching 2,000W), which may drive ~10x content increase if the new
design is actually adopted. This is yet to be confirmed, but we believe the spec
ugprade trend is certain.

executive summary, providing a list of the issues at a glance. These are covered in more detail in subsequent slides. For an overview of key events between

October and December 2026, see our latest 90-day Geopolitical Risk Radar.

### Page 3

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics | October 2026
Deutsche Bank
Research Institute
Diplomacy appears firmly back in play. The past month saw continued fighting and renewed diplomacy.
Reporting suggests that the US is prepared to extend troop deployments in the Middle East to 2027. Iran
targeted both US forces in the region and some tankers moving through the Strait, but has been unable to
prevent a significant rebound in crude flows through Hormuz. On 22 SEP, talks were mediated by Qatar,

Pakistan and Egypt, and on 24 SEP, Iran presented the US with a seven-day roadmap to a ceasefire leading
to a phased reopening of the Strait of Hormuz and a return to nuclear negotiations, conditional on a US
lifting of its blockade of Iran, sanctions relief and broader cessation of hostilities. The Trump
Administration rejected the proposal, but negotiations continue.
To watch will be whether October is the month the war transitions into a negotiated settlement, which

may hinge on whether Iran is willing to make some concessions on sanctions relief and the nuclear
question, and whether Trump is keen to demonstrate that the war has produced a political and economic
result in the final weeks before the 3 NOV midterm elections. It is plausible that the current diplomatic
channel could produce a temporary de-escalation mechanism as long as there is pathway around the
broader nuclear issue.
Both sides will reconvene for trade talks in Beijing on 8-9 October, which the EU views as a deadline for

“tangible results”. Brussels remains focused on its rising trade deficit with China – which has reached a
record €1 billion per day – and the impact of Chinese goods on European industry. The bloc has vowed to
take measures if talks fail.
While both sides could achieve progress in talks, a lasting breakthrough appears improbable, setting the
stage for more trade tensions into 2027. New EU trade investigations, curbs on Chinese imports, and EU-
level instruments intended to address alleged Chinese overcapacity, are possible outcomes, to which

Beijing has stated it would respond.
China-EU trade talks could also impact China-US relations. Should talks with Brussels fall short of averting
increased European trade pressure, we assess that Beijing may further prioritise near-term US stability. See
Rules of Engagement: Mapping the Next Phase of China-US Ties for more.
In the spotlight for October 2026: US-Iran war and EU-China relations
US-Iran war
EU-China trade relations
Strait of Hormuz total liquids exports (mmb/d)

Source: Kpler, Deutsche Bank Research.
Source: Kpler, Deutsche Bank Research. For more, see: Hsueh On Oil:
Success of the shuttle system - Deutsche Bank Research
Bab el Mandeb total liquids exports (mmb/d)

### Page 4

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics | October 2026
Deutsche Bank
Research Institute
Latvia will elect all 100 members of the 15th Saeima. Latvia, which borders Russia and Belarus, has been
among the most vocal supporters of Ukraine. The next government will set the country’s course on
defence spending, Ukraine aid, sanctions enforcement and border security. The field is highly fragmented
with the governing parties lacking a majority in the polls. The fragmentation matters as a weakened and

multi-party scramble could mean a slower, more unstable coalition.
Brazil’s first electoral round is on 4 OCT, with a runoff on 25 OCT. This is a critical election as it will
decide whether Latin America’s biggest economy continues President Lula’s state-led social spending or
pivots to a more fiscally-orthodox agenda under Bolsonaro. Polls have gone from a Lula lead in the spring
to a statistical dead heat between Lula and Bolsonaro. A Brazilian Supreme Court crisis – which has

implicated Supreme Court Justices and politicians on both sides – is weighing on the race. Congressional
and gubernatorial races will also impact the governability of the country.
This election will also shape whether the country hedges between Washington and Beijing under Lula or
tilts toward closer US trade and security alignment under Bolsonaro with potential implications for
tariffs, BRICS+ cohesion, and Latin America’s broader positioning between the two powers.

The meetings will take place in Bangkok, bringing finance ministers, central bankers, policy-makers and
business leaders together. The agenda is expected to centre on global growth, financial stability, debt,
trade and investment, development finance and the impact of AI on jobs and productivity, with particular
attention given to mobilizing private capital. The key watchpoint will be the outlook for global growth
and inflation, debt vulnerabilities, the future of the international financial architecture and how policy-

makers respond to increasing fragmentation in trade and capital flows.
Key events, issues and themes to watch in October 2026
Listed in chronological order
General election in Brazil (4 October)
Source: Waranont (Joe) on Unsplash
Latvian elections (3 October)
Annual meetings of the International Monetary Fund and World Bank (12-18 October)
3

### Page 5

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics | October 2026
Deutsche Bank
Research Institute
Leaders are expected to focus heavily on Ukraine, European security and defence, competitiveness and
the EU’s economic position in a more contested global environment. They will also take forward
negotiations on the 2028-2034 EU long-term budget. They set themselves a soft deadline in June to get
the budget broadly locked down because missing it pushes ratification into 2027 and risks a funding gap

for beneficiaries from JAN 2028. To watch will be a genuine breakthrough on the multiannual framework
or more likely what “substantial” progress may look like. Ukraine funding is the other pressure point. This
meeting will be important for understanding the direction of European defence spending and industrial
policy, the next phase of budget negotiations and the EU response to growing external economic
pressure and strategic dependencies.
The Canadian province of Alberta will hold a referendum, which includes a question about the province’s

future within Canada. This is not an independence vote itself but asking voters to choose between
remaining a Canadian province and asking the Alberta government to begin the constitutional process
required for a future binding referendum on separation. Current polling suggests “remain” is comfortably
ahead. The significance is around potential renewed uncertainty around Canada’s federal cohesion,
energy policy, fiscal transfers and Alberta's relationship with Ottawa and Washington. Even a modest

agreement to begin a process towards secession would likely keep alive equalization grievances as a
structural fault line in Canadian federalism feeding into political risk.
.
The event’s theme focuses on “Advancing Alliances in a New Era.” It will tackle how governments and
businesses respond to a world where geopolitics, economic security, supply chains and technology are
increasingly intertwined. There is likely to be a strong focus on the future of the transatlantic

relationship, including how Europe adapts to a more demanding security environment. There will also be
discussions of tariffs, supply chain resilience, critical capabilities and the weaponization of economic
interdependence. It will align with Chancellor Merz’s efforts to ensure Europe actively shapes outcomes
around strategic autonomy and reducing China exposure in manufacturing.
Key events, issues and themes to watch in October 2026
Listed in chronological order

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics | October 2026
Deutsche Bank

The second summit will take place in Saudi Arabi, bringing EU and Gulf leaders together. The agenda is
expected to focus on regional security and the Middle East, energy and economic security, trade and
investment, infrastructure, technology and broader strategic cooperation. The security dimension will be
particularly salient given the current crisis in the region. Economically, the relationship is substantial: EU-

GCC trade reached €165.6bn in 2025.
To watch will be movement on the long-stalled EU-GCC free trade talks and visa-waiver requests from
Qatar and Saudi Arabia in addition to the extent of the focus on Gaza reconstruction, Iran and regional
security get relative to the economic agenda.
GCC states have consistently used these summits to press for clearer EU positioning on Middle East
conflicts in exchange for economic cooperation. The summit comes amid at a critical juncture where

Europe is recalibrating its external partnerships.
The Central Committee of the Communist Party of China will hold its Fifth Plenary Session in Beijing.
This will be one of the most important events on the country’s political calendar ahead of the 21st Party
Congress in late 2027.
The Plenum will focus on Party governance, and investors will scrutinise signals regarding leadership
changes and personnel reshuffles, institutional reforms, and political priorities for the next Congress.

While fifth plenums historically focus on economic planning, the development guidelines for the 15th
Five-Year Plan (2026–2030) were already adopted during the Fourth Plenum in OCT 2025, allowing the
upcoming session to focus on personnel and governance matters.

Elections will be held for the 26th Knesset, the first since the October 7th attacks and wars in Gaza,
Lebanon and Iran. This is the first Knesset since 1988 to actually complete a full four-year term without
early dissolution. Benjamim Netanyahu remains the dominant single figure but faces a fragmented field:
Naftali Bennett’s new “Together” party is polling as the main rival bloc, alongside Gadi Eisenkot’s Yashar,

Benny Gantz’s Blue and White, and the further right Ben Gvir and Smotrich factions.
Israel’s proportional system means the election is effectively about coalition formation. Key faultlines to
watch: the ultra-Orthodox draft-exemption fight, how the war’s legacy and hostage/reconstruction issues
in Gaza factor into campaign messaging, and whether a Netanyahu-led bloc can reassemble a working
majority or whether the Bennett/Eisenkot-style “anyone but Bibi” coalitions gain enough traction. The

outcome will shape Israel’s posture on critical regional issues, including Gaza reconstruction, Iran policy
and West Bank settlement activity.
This summit is shaping up as a particularly important juncture against a more ambitious push to redefine
the relationship. During Mark Carney’s SEP visit to Strasbourg, he called for a comprehensive strategic
alliance with Europe. European Commission President von der Leyen went further, proposing that Canada

become the EU’s first “associate member”.
The agenda is expected to centre on trade and economic security, defense, critical minerals, energy,
digital cooperation and supply-chain resilience, building on the EU-Canada security and Defense
Partnership in the EU’s SAFE defense initiative. The summit will be watched for concrete progress on a
digital trade agreement and broader economic cooperation.

The foreign ministers’ meeting comes ahead of the December Leaders’ Summit in Miami under the US’
chairmanship of the G20 this year. Trump’s G20 year has organized around four expert tracks – trade,
innovation, energy and deregulation. The session is framed around economic prosperity, energy security
and technological innovation, a much more transactional focus than previous foreign ministerials, which

typically focused on geopolitical crisis management.
It will be a test of whether the G20 can sustain cooperation amid intensifying geopolitical fragmentation.
In addition, Russia has confirmed its intent to actively participate in the meeting. This would further
normalize Russia’s multilateral engagement. For Europe and other partners, a key question will be the
extent to which the G20 can be a forum for broader international cooperation.

## Document 322: 「関西の景気動向」９月号 AI関連需要の拡大と関西の製造業生産（PDF：841KB）

Source PDF: all report pdf/「関西の景気動向」９月号　AI関連需要の拡大と関西の製造業生産（PDF：841KB）.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

2026 年９月 
株式会社 日本総合研究所 
調査部 関西経済研究センター 
http://www.jri.co.jp/report/medium/kansai/

１．景気の現状と展望 
 関西の景気は、緩やかな回復基調を維持しつつも、一部に弱い動きがみられる。企業部門では、円安傾向に
加えて、世界的なAI関連需要の拡大を背景に輸出額は増加している。AI関連投資の活発化などを背景に、
鉱工業生産にも持ち直しの動きがみられる。設備投資は、中東情勢の混乱などによる先行き不透明感が意識
されるなかでも、企業の収益計画が高めの水準を維持するもとで拡大基調が続いている。一方、家計部門では、
所得環境の改善が続くものの物価高が重石となり、個人消費の回復ペースは緩慢にとどまっている。 
先行きを展望すると、中東情勢の混乱が長引くなか、資源価格の高止まりが企業活動の重石となる。一方、
世界的な AI 関連需要の拡大が関西の生産や輸出に波及する動きが続き、設備投資の押し上げにもつながる
と見込まれる。個人消費は、持続的な賃上げの動きが下支えに寄与するものの、物価高による下押し圧力が
根強く、回復ペースは引き続き緩慢にとどまる見通しである。

２．個別指標の動き 
（１） 輸出・生産動向 
関西の輸出額（円ベース）は大きく増加している。国・地域別にみると、中国（香港を含む）向けや、アジ
ア（除く中国・香港）向けの伸びが大きく、ともに半導体等電子部品の増加がけん引している。米国向けは振
れを伴いつつも増加傾向にある。品目別にみると、電気機器では半導体等電子部品、一般機械では半導体
等製造装置の増加が顕著にみられ、世界的な AI 関連需要の高まりが押し上げに寄与しているとみられる。金
属及び同製品も先端材料としての需要増や、それに伴う市況の高騰を背景に高水準で推移している。 
一方、輸出を数量面でみると、回復傾向にあるものの、ペースは緩やかにとどまっている。中国向けやアジア
NIEs 向けは低水準での推移が続いているほか、ASEAN 向けも伸び悩んでいる。米国向けは振れを伴いつつ
も、基調としては底堅く推移している。日本銀行が公表している実質輸出指数にも増勢の加速はみられない。
